Complete reference: 201 terms and shapes · All vocabularies and reading guide

The Binary Data Description Ontology (BDDO) provides a formal vocabulary to declaratively describe the structure, syntax, and layout of binary data. It is the foundational physical layer of the Hexplain Ontology Family. BDDO allows authors to model binary formats as nested structures and typed fields, with support for dynamic layouts through conditional logic, variable sizing, offset-based addressing, bit-level fields, arrays, stream synchronization, enumerations, and integrity checksums. This specification provides the normative OWL vocabulary for BDDO and a set of SHACL shapes for validating BDDO descriptions.

This document is an unofficial draft produced for the purpose of defining the Hexplain standard. It has no official standing and is not endorsed by any standards body.

Introduction

The primary goal of the Hexplain project is to make opaque binary data machine-understandable. The first step is to provide a standardized, unambiguous way to describe the physical structure of the data. The Binary Data Description Ontology (BDDO) serves this purpose.

BDDO provides a set of RDF classes and properties that can be used to create a graph-based model of a binary format. This model can then be used by a conforming Hexplain processor (see the Processing Model specification) to parse a binary stream into a structured, logical representation, and ultimately into an RDF instance graph.

Modeling Approach: Vocabulary vs. Constraints

BDDO follows a deliberate separation of concerns that is a recommended ontology-engineering practice:

  • The OWL vocabulary declares terms (classes, properties, individuals) with human-readable metadata (rdfs:label, rdfs:comment, rdfs:isDefinedBy), lightweight semantics (rdfs:subClassOf, alignment axioms), and each property's rdfs:range (its value type). It deliberately does not assert rdfs:domain, because a domain axiom would silently type any subject of a property — a term is often reused across several classes.
  • The SHACL shapes carry the remaining constraints — the classes a property may apply to (its "domain"), value cardinalities, and any refinement of the declared ranges (controlled-vocabulary membership, numeric bounds). Validation therefore never mutates the graph, and errors are reported rather than repaired by inference.

Conformance

The key words "MUST", "MUST NOT", "REQUIRED", "SHALL", "SHALL NOT", "SHOULD", "SHOULD NOT", "RECOMMENDED", "MAY", and "OPTIONAL" in this document are to be interpreted as described in [[RFC2119]].

A validator checking a BDDO description graph against § Normative SHACL Shapes MUST either include the BDDO rdfs:subClassOf axioms of § Normative OWL Vocabulary in the graph it validates, or apply RDFS subclass closure to that graph before validating. SHACL selects the targets of sh:targetClass through rdf:type/rdfs:subClassOf*, so a validator that omits those axioms silently under-validates every subclass: a bddo:KeyValueHeader, for example, would not be reached by bddo:StructShape and its bddo:endianness would go unchecked.

Core Concepts

The BDDO model is built upon the following classes.

bddo:Struct
A composite structure or container for other elements. A Struct is an ordered sequence of Fields. Examples: a file header, a network packet, a PNG chunk.
bddo:Field
A single element within a Struct. A Field may represent one value, a bit-level slice, or an array of values, and is associated with a data type.
bddo:DataType
The type of data a Field contains. BDDO defines core primitive data types (e.g. bddo:uint32be). This class can be extended in profiles to create user-defined type aliases (e.g. media:fourCC).
bddo:DataTypeRule
A single rule for conditionally selecting a data type for a field, consisting of a condition and the data type to use when the condition holds.
bddo:EndiannessRule
A single rule for conditionally selecting a byte order for a Struct or Field, consisting of a condition and the endianness to use when the condition holds. Used by formats that declare their own byte order in a magic or version field.
bddo:Enumeration / bddo:EnumValue
A mapping of raw parsed values to named symbols, so that (for example) a colour-type byte of 2 can be surfaced as RGB in the output graph and referenced by name in conditions.
bddo:Checksum
A declarative description of an integrity check: the algorithm and the byte range it covers. A field that stores a checksum value links to it so that a processor can verify data integrity.

Sequencing, Addressing & Alignment

By default, the Fields listed in bddo:hasField are parsed sequentially and contiguously: each field begins where the previous one ended. Many real formats (TIFF/GeoTIFF IFDs, ZIP central directories, ELF section tables, PDF cross-reference tables) instead locate structures by offset. BDDO supports both models.

bddo:atOffset, bddo:atOffsetFromField, bddo:atOffsetFromExpression
Relocate a Field or Struct to a fixed offset, an offset read from another field, or an offset computed by a HEL expression, respectively. A field with an offset does not advance the sequential cursor for its siblings unless the processor is told otherwise.
bddo:offsetBase
The reference point the offset is measured from: bddo:streamStart (default), bddo:streamEnd (for formats parsed from the tail, such as ZIP), bddo:parentStart, or bddo:currentPosition.
bddo:alignment
An xsd:positiveInteger. Before parsing the field, the processor MUST advance the cursor to the next multiple of this value (padding bytes are skipped).

Bit-Level Fields

Formats such as IPv4, TCP, and many compression headers pack several logical values into a single byte. A Field MAY declare bddo:bitLength (an xsd:positiveInteger number of bits). Consecutive bit-length fields are packed into the underlying bytes; the packing order is governed by bddo:bitOrder (bddo:MSBFirst, the default, or bddo:LSBFirst) declared on the enclosing Struct. A run of bit fields SHOULD align to a byte boundary; a processor MUST pad to the next byte before parsing a subsequent non-bit field.

Delimited Text Records

Record- and line-oriented text — key = value headers, CSV, whitespace-separated grids — cannot be addressed by byte offset. bddo:DelimitedRecords is a bddo:Struct whose children are located by delimiter position instead: bddo:recordDelimiter splits the byte run into records, and bddo:fieldDelimiter splits each record into values. Its two specialisations cover the common cases — bddo:KeyValueHeader, where each field is matched by bddo:key rather than position, and bddo:DelimitedTable, where bddo:hasField gives an ordered column list that repeats per row.

A field of a bddo:DelimitedRecords takes its extent from its container's delimiters, so it declares no bddo:size and no bddo:terminator of its own; restating a delimiter as a field's terminator is redundant and breaks when a column is inserted. Such fields are therefore exempt from the sizing requirements that apply to variable-length and ASCII-coded numeric fields elsewhere.

Column-aligned text needs a different separation rule. In an ASCII grid a key and its value, or two adjacent numbers, are parted by a run of spaces whose width exists only to line the columns up — ncols 100. A fixed bddo:fieldDelimiter would read each of those spaces as another separator and yield a row of empty values, so bddo:whitespaceSeparated says instead that a run of one or more whitespace bytes, of any length, separates exactly two values. Leading and trailing whitespace on a record is ignored. The two rules are mutually exclusive.

Omitting bddo:recordDelimiter as well makes the content one flat stream of values rather than rows. That is how an ASCII grid whose rows wrap freely across lines is read: line breaks carry no meaning, and the grid's shape comes from the field's dlv:DataLayout or repeat count.

This primitive is deliberately non-recursive. It describes flat delimited text only; nested grammars such as XML and JSON are outside BDDO's scope by design.

Core Data Types

BDDO provides a normative set of built-in primitive data types. All conforming processors MUST support these types. Each is an instance of bddo:DataType and carries a normative bddo:xsdType that fixes the RDF literal datatype produced when a value of that type is emitted into the output graph.

Integer and floating types come in three forms: an endianness-neutral form (e.g. bddo:uint32) that inherits its byte order from the enclosing Struct or Field via bddo:endianness, and explicit big-endian / little-endian forms (e.g. bddo:uint32be, bddo:uint32le). Single-byte types have no byte order.

Tree Documents

A great many formats wrap a payload that BDDO can already describe in a label it could not read at all: PDS4, DIMAP, SAFE, Sentinel-2, RadarSat-2, TerraSAR-X and CPHD are all an XML manifest over a clean binary array, and Zarr's chunk store was fully covered by chunked layout and encoding pipelines while .zarray — being JSON — kept it out of reach. A bddo:TreeDocument is a Struct whose fields are located by a path into a parsed node tree.

BDDO does not describe the syntax of these documents. It addresses nodes in the tree a conforming parser produces. That distinction is what keeps this from turning into an XML specification: the tree is someone else's output, and BDDO's only job is to say which node supplies which field.

bddo:treeSyntaxbddo:nodePath is…Example
bddo:xmlAn abbreviated XPath: child steps, attribute steps, positional predicates (1-based). No axes, no functions, no comparison predicates./pds:Product/pds:Array_2D_Image/pds:Axis_Array[1]/pds:elements
bddo:jsonA JSON Pointer [[RFC6901]]: member names and array indices (0-based)./shape/0, /compressor/id

Both are adopted rather than invented. JSON Pointer already specifies how to escape / and ~ inside a member name, and is already implemented everywhere; the XPath subset is deliberately small because a description states where a value is, and a path that can compute is a query engine's problem rather than a description's. The two index conventions differ — XPath predicates are 1-based, JSON Pointer indices 0-based — and are kept as their standards define them, because normalising to one would surprise everyone who knows either.

XML paths need their prefixes bound with bddo:hasNamespaceBinding, and this is not optional in practice: PDS4, DIMAP and SAFE are all namespaced, and an unprefixed path against a namespaced document matches nothing rather than failing. The prefix is matched to the document's own namespace declarations rather than to whatever prefix the document happens to spell them with. An empty prefix binds the default namespace, which an abbreviated XPath cannot otherwise reach — an unprefixed step means "no namespace", not "the default one". A binding on a JSON document is rejected, since JSON Pointer has no prefixes and such a binding signals a misunderstanding of what the paths below will match.

A path selecting no node leaves the field absent; one selecting several is an error unless the field repeats, in which case its value is the selected nodes in document order. Every field of a tree document is located by exactly one bddo:nodePath and by nothing else — a byte offset or a record key here is a leftover from another container form and would address nothing.

Nested Records

A bddo:DelimitedRecords is flat unless it declares bddo:hasGrouping. That was enough for ESRI .hdr, ENVI and their kin, but stopped at the label formats that are otherwise the same shape — PVL/ODL, the ERMapper header, INI-style labels — for want of a closing token. Those labels always sat over a payload that was already a clean array; only the label could not be decomposed.

A bddo:RecordGrouping names one of three styles and the two tokens that delimit a group. Three styles rather than one general grammar, because each is fully described by two tokens plus where the group's NAME comes from — and a general nested-grammar surface would be a parser generator, which this is not.

bddo:groupingStyleOpens / closesGroup nameExample
bddo:keyedGroupA record whose key is the tokenThe opening record's valueOBJECT = IMAGE … END_OBJECT (PVL/ODL)
bddo:valuedGroupA record whose value is the tokenThe opening record's keyCoordinateSpace Begin … CoordinateSpace End (ERMapper)
bddo:sectionHeadedA record bracketed by the two tokens; the next one implicitly closes itThe text between the tokens[OVERVIEW] (INI)

Both tokens are always required. A grouping that says where a group starts but not where it ends cannot be read at all — every record after the first group would be inside it. bddo:sectionHeaded nests one deep: a section is ended by the next one, so sections cannot contain sections. That is what the style permits, not a limitation of this model.

Inside a grouped container a field is located by bddo:keyPath — the group names and finally the key, joined by bddo:pathSeparator (default /, declared on the container because a format whose group names contain a slash must choose something else). bddo:keyPath and bddo:key are mutually exclusive: a field cannot be found two ways, and a description asserting both would leave a reader to guess which wins. A path in a container that does not nest is rejected by bddo:KeyPathShape, because it would otherwise silently match nothing rather than fail.

Variable-Length Integers

An integer whose WIDTH is discovered from its own bytes rather than declared. Framing such a field was always possible byte by byte; reconstructing the VALUE was not, which is what kept protobuf-framed and SQLite-backed formats out of reach.

IRIEncodingxsdType
bddo:varuintUnsigned base-128 (LEB128): seven value bits per byte, little-endian, high bit set on every byte but the last. Protobuf's plain varint.xsd:nonNegativeInteger
bddo:varintLEB128 carrying a zigzag-encoded value, so small negatives stay short. Protobuf's sint32/sint64. Distinct from reading a varuint and reinterpreting the bits, which sign-extends instead.xsd:integer
bddo:sqliteVarintOne to nine BIG-endian bytes, high bit as continuation for the first eight, the ninth contributing all eight bits. A different scheme from LEB128, not a dialect of it; used by GeoPackage, MBTiles and Rasterlite.xsd:integer

A field of one of these types MUST NOT declare a width or terminator — bddo:size, bddo:sizeFromField, bddo:sizeFromExpression, bddo:sizeToEndOfStream, bddo:terminator or bddo:bitLength — because its length comes from the data and a declared one could contradict it. bddo:VarintWidthShape enforces this.

ASCII-Coded Numeric Types

Many record-oriented formats write numbers as text in fixed-width fields — NITF BCS-N, FITS card values, USGS DEM Fortran fields. bddo:asciiInteger and bddo:asciiDecimal read such a field as a number rather than a string, so its value may drive bddo:sizeFromExpression, bddo:atOffsetFromExpression and bddo:repeatCountFromExpression. The field's width comes from bddo:size (or a terminator), except for a field of a bddo:DelimitedRecords, whose extent comes from its container's delimiters; bddo:numericBase selects the radix.

Properties

Structural Properties

bddo:hasField
Links a bddo:Struct to its ordered components. The object MUST be an rdf:List of bddo:Field to preserve order.
bddo:endianness
The byte order for a bddo:Struct, bddo:Field, or bddo:DataType. Value MUST be bddo:BigEndian or bddo:LittleEndian. The nearest declaration wins (DataType > Field > Struct > inherited from the parent).
bddo:hasConditionalEndianness
Ordered list of bddo:EndiannessRule; the first rule whose bddo:condition holds sets the byte order for this term and its descendants. For formats that declare their own byte order. Mutually exclusive with bddo:endianness.
bddo:bitOrder
The bit-packing order for bit-level fields in a Struct. Value MUST be bddo:MSBFirst or bddo:LSBFirst.
bddo:syncOnMarker
A byte sequence a processor MUST scan for before parsing this bddo:Struct. Value MUST be an xsd:hexBinary literal.
bddo:size / bddo:sizeFromField / bddo:sizeFromExpression (on a Struct)
Bounds the Struct's total byte extent, establishing a bounded region for its children: descendant reads MUST NOT cross it, bddo:sizeToEndOfStream resolves to the region end, and trailing/padding bytes are skipped. Essential for length-delimited containers (MP4/ISOBMFF boxes, EBML, chunk payloads). At most one mechanism.

Field Properties

bddo:dataType
The Field's default data type. Value MUST be a bddo:DataType or a bddo:Struct. A Field MUST have either a bddo:dataType or a bddo:hasConditionalDataType (or both).
bddo:hasConditionalDataType
An rdf:List of bddo:DataTypeRule allowing the processor to select a more specific data type at runtime.
bddo:hasDispatchTable
A bddo:DispatchTable selecting the Field's type by key: the parsed value of bddo:dispatchOnField (or the value of bddo:dispatchOnExpression) is compared by equality with the bddo:armKey of every bddo:DispatchArm whose bddo:armTable is this table; the matching arm's bddo:armDataType is the effective type, else bddo:dispatchDefault, else the Field's bddo:dataType. Arms are independent subjects, not list members, so a description that owl:imports another may add arms to its tables — the mechanism for open type registries (container box types, tagged extensions). Keys MUST be unique per table across all contributing graphs. Mutually exclusive with bddo:hasConditionalDataType, which remains the form for ordered, possibly overlapping predicate rules.
bddo:seekScope
On an offset-addressed Field: bddo:regionScope (default) confines the read to the innermost bounded region; bddo:streamScope resolves the target against the whole stream and suspends the region bound for that one read. Needed when a table inside one length-delimited container locates payloads stored in another.
bddo:size / bddo:sizeFromField / bddo:sizeFromExpression / bddo:sizeToEndOfStream
The size of the field in bytes: fixed, read from another field, computed by a HEL expression, or "consume the remainder of the stream" (xsd:boolean). A Field MUST use at most one sizing mechanism, and variable-length types (bddo:bytes, bddo:string) MUST use exactly one sizing or termination mechanism — except for a field of a bddo:DelimitedRecords, which is exempt because its extent is set by its container's delimiters.
bddo:terminator
A byte sequence terminating a variable-length field. Value MUST be an xsd:hexBinary literal.
bddo:trimNull
For a fixed-size (padded) bddo:string, the emitted value ends at the first 0x00 within the field's width (NUL-padded names, e.g. TAR/ZIP).
bddo:encoding
The character encoding of a bddo:string field. Value MUST be one of the encoding individuals (bddo:utf8, bddo:ascii, bddo:utf16le, bddo:utf16be, bddo:latin1). Defaults to bddo:utf8.
bddo:numericBase
Radix (8, 10 or 16) for reading a bddo:asciiInteger field. Default 10.
bddo:bitLength
The width, in bits, of a sub-byte field (see Bit-Level Fields).
bddo:repeatCount / bddo:repeatCountFromField / bddo:repeatCountFromExpression / bddo:repeatUntil
Turn the field into an array parsed a fixed number of times, a number read from another field, a number computed by a HEL expression, or repeatedly until a HEL boolean expression evaluates to true. A Field MUST use at most one repetition mechanism.
bddo:atOffset / bddo:atOffsetFromField / bddo:atOffsetFromExpression / bddo:offsetBase / bddo:alignment
Addressing and alignment (see Sequencing, Addressing & Alignment).
bddo:hasFixedValue
A constant expected value, used to validate markers and magic numbers. The literal is compared against the parsed value after decoding according to the field's data type.
bddo:isPresentIf
A HEL boolean expression; the field is present only if it evaluates to true.
bddo:valueFromExpression
Declares a derived, zero-byte field: its value is computed by a HEL expression rather than read from the stream. The field consumes no bytes; its computed value is placed in the parse context under the field's name so later sizing, offset, presence, mapping and validation expressions may reference it. Such a field declares no bddo:dataType and no sizing, offset or bit-length; the result's type is that of the HEL expression.
bddo:validIf
A HEL boolean expression that MUST hold once this field is parsed; if it evaluates to false the parse fails with a validation error. Use for post-parse constraints such as a bound on a count. Richer, graph-level constraints belong in SHACL at the semantic layer.
bddo:enumeration
Links a Field to a bddo:Enumeration that maps its raw value to a named symbol. If the Enumeration sets bddo:enumIsFlags true, the raw value is a bitmask and every matching flag symbol is emitted.
bddo:checksum
Links a Field (the one storing the checksum value) to a bddo:Checksum describing the algorithm and covered range.
bddo:recordDelimiter / bddo:fieldDelimiter / bddo:keyValueSeparator
On a bddo:DelimitedRecords (see Delimited Text Records): the byte sequence splitting the byte run into records, splitting a record into values, and (in a bddo:KeyValueHeader) splitting a key from its value. Each value MUST be an xsd:hexBinary literal.
bddo:quoteChar / bddo:escapeChar
On a bddo:DelimitedRecords: the byte that quotes a value so it may contain a delimiter, and the byte that escapes the following byte. Values MUST be xsd:hexBinary literals.
bddo:commentPrefix / bddo:skipRecords / bddo:trimWhitespace
On a bddo:DelimitedRecords: a leading string marking a record as a comment to ignore, a count of leading records to discard before parsing, and whether to strip leading/trailing whitespace from every key and value.
bddo:key / bddo:keyIsCaseInsensitive
On a Field of a bddo:KeyValueHeader: the key naming the field, which replaces positional location, and whether key matching ignores ASCII case.

Data Type, Rule, Enumeration & Checksum Properties

bddo:baseType
The fundamental kind of data. Value MUST be one of bddo:baseInteger, bddo:baseFloat, bddo:baseString, or bddo:baseBytes.
bddo:bitWidth, bddo:isSigned, bddo:xsdType
Describe a bddo:DataType: its width in bits, its signedness, and the RDF literal datatype produced on output.
bddo:condition, bddo:ruleDataType
A bddo:DataTypeRule's HEL condition and the data type (a bddo:DataType or bddo:Struct) to apply when it holds.
bddo:condition, bddo:ruleEndianness
A bddo:EndiannessRule's HEL condition and the byte order (bddo:BigEndian or bddo:LittleEndian) to apply when it holds.
bddo:hasEnumValue, bddo:enumRawValue, bddo:enumSymbol
An bddo:Enumeration's members; each bddo:EnumValue pairs a raw literal (bddo:enumRawValue) with an optional symbolic IRI (bddo:enumSymbol) and/or rdfs:label.
bddo:checksumAlgorithm, bddo:coversFromField, bddo:coversToField, bddo:coversFromExpression, bddo:coversToExpression
A bddo:Checksum's algorithm and the byte range it protects. The algorithm is one of bddo:crc16, bddo:crc32, bddo:adler32, bddo:md5, bddo:sha1 or bddo:sha256 — a closed set, since a checksum names an algorithm a reader must already implement to verify anything. Each end of the range is stated independently, as a field reference or a HEL expression, so a range may start at a named field and end at a computed offset. The field form is inclusive of coversToField's last byte; the expression form is the half-open [start,end). (bddo:coversExpression, a single expression for the whole range, is deprecated in favour of the pair.)

Complete term and shape reference

201 named resources are documented below. Definitions and usage notes come from the canonical RDF; range and validation facts are rendered from its axioms and shapes. RDFS/OWL inference, SHACL validation, and editorial usage guidance are shown separately. Blank-node property shapes and logical alternatives are expanded under their owning named shapes.

Constraint language follows W3C SHACL; publication and persistent term identifiers follow the W3C vocabulary publication guidance. Concept definitions and scope notes use SKOS documentation properties.

Label
Binary Data Description Ontology (BDDO)
Definition

bddo:AsciiNumericWidthShape — Ascii Numeric Width Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#AsciiNumericWidthShape

Label
Ascii Numeric Width Shape
Definition
A reusable validation contract for Ascii Numeric Width. A bddo:asciiInteger / bddo:asciiDecimal field must declare a width (size / sizeFromField / sizeFromExpression) or a terminator.
Usage scope
Validation activation: instances of bddo:Field Constraints apply only within that activation or through shape references; they are not global OWL domain axioms.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range
Not applicable to this node shape. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Activation
instances of bddo:Field
Severity
SHACL default: sh:Violation. This applies to results of this shape; nested property shapes have their own severity (default sh:Violation), rather than inheriting it. Rules generate triples instead of validation results.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Constraint and rule specification
  • rdf:type: sh:NodeShape
  • SPARQL validation constraint:
    • Validation message: "A bddo:asciiInteger / bddo:asciiDecimal field must declare a width (size / sizeFromField / sizeFromExpression) or a terminator."
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: bddo:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT $this WHERE {
            $this bddo:dataType ?dt .
            FILTER(?dt IN (bddo:asciiInteger, bddo:asciiDecimal))
            FILTER NOT EXISTS { $this bddo:size ?s }
            FILTER NOT EXISTS { $this bddo:sizeFromField ?f }
            FILTER NOT EXISTS { $this bddo:sizeFromExpression ?e }
            FILTER NOT EXISTS { $this bddo:terminator ?t }
            FILTER NOT EXISTS {
                ?p bddo:hasField/rdf:rest*/rdf:first $this .
                ?p a ?c .
                # Same exemption as bddo:VariableLengthFieldShape, and it must be kept in step
                # with it: both ask "does the container decide this field's extent?", and a new
                # container form has to be listed in both or a description conforms to one shape
                # and not the other.
                FILTER(?c IN (bddo:DelimitedRecords, bddo:KeyValueHeader, bddo:DelimitedTable,
                              bddo:TreeDocument))
            }
        }
  • Target class: bddo:Field

Class

bddo:Checksum — Checksum

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#Checksum

Label
Checksum
Definition
A checksum/hash over a byte range. Each END of the range is stated independently: a field reference (coversFromField/coversToField) or a HEL expression yielding a byte offset (coversFromExpression/coversToExpression). The two ends may use different mechanisms -- a checksum covering everything from a named header field to a length carried elsewhere needs exactly that. Note the boundary convention differs by mechanism, because each follows what its inputs naturally give: the field form runs from the FIRST byte of coversFromField to the LAST byte of coversToField (inclusive), while the expression form is the half-open [start,end) that the two offsets delimit.
Usage scope
Use in physical binary-format descriptions. Extents and offsets describe bytes unless a term explicitly specifies bits; HEL evaluation and failure behavior follow the processing specification. Assert this class on a resource representing the defined entity; subclass links below are the asserted hierarchy, not merely similarity links.
Declared RDF type
owl:Class
Domain / range
Not applicable to this class. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Shape usage / validation context
bddo:ChecksumShape, bddo:FieldShape
Cardinality
Context-dependent. The linked shapes state minimum/maximum counts at their value paths; no universal cardinality is inferred.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Declared axioms and values
  • rdf:type: owl:Class

bddo:ConditionalEndiannessShape — Conditional Endianness Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#ConditionalEndiannessShape

Label
Conditional Endianness Shape
Definition
A reusable validation contract for bddo:endianness, bddo:hasConditionalEndianness. Every member of a hasConditionalEndianness list must be a bddo:EndiannessRule. bddo:endianness and bddo:hasConditionalEndianness are mutually exclusive. bddo:hasConditionalEndianness must point to a single rdf:List.
Usage scope
Validation activation: subjects using bddo:hasConditionalEndianness Constraints apply only within that activation or through shape references; they are not global OWL domain axioms.

    • Required value class: bddo:EndiannessRule

    • Value path:
      1. bddo:hasConditionalEndianness

      3. rdf:first

Label
Delimited Records
Definition

bddo:DispatchTable — Dispatch Table

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#DispatchTable

Label
Dispatch Table
Definition
A keyed, open selection of a field's effective type: the dispatch key is matched by equality against the keys of arms that any importing description may contribute.
Usage scope
Use in physical binary-format descriptions. Extents and offsets describe bytes unless a term explicitly specifies bits; HEL evaluation and failure behavior follow the processing specification. Assert this class on a resource representing the defined entity; subclass links below are the asserted hierarchy, not merely similarity links.
Declared RDF type
owl:Class
Domain / range
Not applicable to this class. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Shape usage / validation context
bddo:DispatchArmShape, bddo:DispatchKeyUniquenessShape, bddo:DispatchTableShape, bddo:FieldShape
Cardinality
Context-dependent. The linked shapes state minimum/maximum counts at their value paths; no universal cardinality is inferred.
Additional source notes
A keyed, open selection of a Field's effective type. The value of the key (dispatchOnField or dispatchOnExpression) is matched by equality against the armKey of the table's DispatchArms; the matching arm's armDataType is the effective type, else dispatchDefault, else the Field's dataType. Arms are independent resources rather than list members, so a description that imports this one can contribute an arm without rewriting anything. Contrast hasConditionalDataType, whose ordered predicate rules are closed and may overlap.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Declared axioms and values
  • rdf:type: owl:Class

bddo:TreeDocumentShape — Tree Document Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#TreeDocumentShape

Label
Tree Document Shape
Definition
A reusable validation contract for bddo:atOffset, bddo:hasField, bddo:hasNamespaceBinding, bddo:key, bddo:keyPath, bddo:nodePath, bddo:treeSyntax. A bddo:TreeDocument must declare at least one field. A bddo:TreeDocument must declare exactly one bddo:treeSyntax: bddo:xml or bddo:json. Every field of a bddo:TreeDocument is located by exactly one bddo:nodePath, and not by a key, a key path or a byte offset. bddo:hasNamespaceBinding applies to an XML tree document; JSON Pointer has no namespace prefixes.
Usage scope
Validation activation: instances of bddo:TreeDocument Constraints apply only within that activation or through shape references; they are not global OWL domain axioms.

      2. bddo:json
    • Maximum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>

    • Minimum value count: "1"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#integer>

      3. rdf:first

    • SPARQL prefix declarations: bddo:_prefixes

            $this bddo:treeSyntax bddo:json .

bddo:VariableLengthFieldShape — Variable Length Field Shape

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#VariableLengthFieldShape

Label
Variable Length Field Shape
Definition
A reusable validation contract for Variable Length Field. A bddo:bytes or bddo:string field must declare a sizing or termination mechanism.
Usage scope
Validation activation: instances of bddo:Field Constraints apply only within that activation or through shape references; they are not global OWL domain axioms.
Declared RDF type
sh:NodeShape
Domain / range
Not applicable to this node shape. Domain and range describe predicates; the types, relationships and constraints below describe this resource.
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Activation
instances of bddo:Field
Severity
SHACL default: sh:Violation. This applies to results of this shape; nested property shapes have their own severity (default sh:Violation), rather than inheriting it. Rules generate triples instead of validation results.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl
Constraint and rule specification
  • rdf:type: sh:NodeShape
  • SPARQL validation constraint:
    • Validation message: "A bddo:bytes or bddo:string field must declare a sizing or termination mechanism."
    • SPARQL prefix declarations: bddo:_prefixes
    • SELECT query returning violations: 
      SELECT $this WHERE {
            $this bddo:dataType ?dt . FILTER(?dt IN (bddo:bytes, bddo:string))
            FILTER NOT EXISTS {
                $this ?p ?v .
                FILTER(?p IN (bddo:size, bddo:sizeFromField, bddo:sizeFromExpression,
                              bddo:sizeToEndOfStream, bddo:terminator))
            }
            FILTER NOT EXISTS {
                ?p bddo:hasField/rdf:rest*/rdf:first $this .
                ?p a ?c .
                # Every container form that determines a field's extent ITSELF -- a delimited
                # record ends at its delimiter, a tree node at its own boundary -- so a declared
                # width would be a second, contradictory answer. Enumerated rather than inferred
                # via rdfs:subClassOf, because these shapes run without inference; a new
                # container form has to be added here too.
                FILTER(?c IN (bddo:DelimitedRecords, bddo:KeyValueHeader, bddo:DelimitedTable,
                              bddo:TreeDocument))
            }
        }
  • Target class: bddo:Field

bddo:coversToExpression — covers to expression

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#coversToExpression

Label
covers to expression
Definition
HEL expression evaluating to the covered range's END byte offset, EXCLUSIVE (one past the last covered byte). Alternative to coversToField for the same bound -- note coversToField names a field whose last byte is included, so the two forms do not take the same number.
Usage scope
Use in physical binary-format descriptions. Extents and offsets describe bytes unless a term explicitly specifies bits; HEL evaluation and failure behavior follow the processing specification. Use it as a predicate. Applicable SHACL paths and their focus-node scopes are listed below; no additional global domain is inferred from that usage.

Use in physical binary-format descriptions. Extents and offsets describe bytes unless a term explicitly specifies bits; HEL evaluation and failure behavior follow the processing specification. Use it as a predicate. Applicable SHACL paths and their focus-node scopes are listed below; no additional global domain is inferred from that usage.

bddo:hasConditionalEndianness — has conditional endianness

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#hasConditionalEndianness

Label
has conditional endianness
Definition
Ordered rdf:List of EndiannessRule. The first rule whose condition holds selects the byte order for this Struct or Field and, as with bddo:endianness, for its descendants unless they override it. Use for formats that declare their own byte order, such as TIFF's 4949h/4D4Dh marker. Mutually exclusive with bddo:endianness.
Usage scope
Use in physical binary-format descriptions. Extents and offsets describe bytes unless a term explicitly specifies bits; HEL evaluation and failure behavior follow the processing specification. bddo:keyPath — key path

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#keyPath

Label
key path
Definition
Locates a field inside nested groups, as group names and finally the key, joined by pathSeparator: 'IMAGE/BAND/NAME'. Mutually exclusive with bddo:key, which locates a field in a flat container -- a field cannot be found two ways, and a description that said both would leave a reader to guess which wins.
Usage scope

bddo:keyedGroup — keyed group

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#keyedGroup

Label
keyed group
Definition
A record whose KEY equals groupOpenToken opens a group, and its VALUE names it; a record whose key equals groupCloseToken closes the innermost open group. PVL/ODL: OBJECT = IMAGE ... END_OBJECT = IMAGE. The closing record MAY repeat the name; a description need not say so, because the name is already known from the opening record.
Usage scope
Use in physical binary-format descriptions. Extents and offsets describe bytes unless a term explicitly specifies bits; HEL evaluation and failure behavior follow the processing specification. Use this IRI as a controlled value where the profile or a shape accepts its declared type.

Additional source notes
For an offset-addressed Field, whether the read may leave the innermost bounded region (streamScope) or is confined to it (regionScope, the default). The region bound is suspended only for that one read. Used where a table inside one length-delimited container locates payloads stored in another.

bddo:sqliteVarint — sqliteVarint

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#sqliteVarint

Label
sqliteVarint
Definition
SQLite's variable-length integer: one to nine BIG-endian bytes, high bit as continuation for the first eight, the ninth contributing all eight bits. A different scheme from LEB128, not a dialect of it; used by SQLite-backed formats (GeoPackage, MBTiles, Rasterlite).

bddo:validIf — valid if

valid if
Definition
A HEL boolean expression that MUST hold once this field is parsed; if it evaluates false the parse fails with a validation error. Use for post-parse constraints such as a magic number or a bound on a count. Richer, graph-level constraints belong in SHACL at the semantic layer.

Datatype property

bddo:valueFromExpression — value from expression

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#valueFromExpression

Label
value from expression
Definition
A HEL expression giving the value of a derived, zero-byte field. The field consumes no bytes; its computed value is placed in the parse context under the field's name so later sizing, offset, presence, mapping and validation expressions may reference it. A field with bddo:valueFromExpression declares no bddo:dataType and no sizing, offset or bit-length; the result's type is that of the HEL expression.
Usage scope
Use in physical binary-format descriptions. Extents and offsets describe bytes unless a term explicitly specifies bits; HEL evaluation and failure behavior follow the processing specification. Use it as a predicate. Applicable SHACL paths and their focus-node scopes are listed below; no additional global domain is inferred from that usage.
Declared RDF type
owl:DatatypeProperty

No global domain declared. SHACL usage scopes below do not create a global domain axiom.

bddo:varint — varint

Label
varint
Definition
Signed base-128 varint: LEB128 carrying a zigzag-encoded value, so small negatives stay short -- n maps to (n << 1) XOR (n >> 63). Protobuf's sint32/sint64. Distinct from reading an unsigned varint and reinterpreting the bits, which is protobuf's int32 and sign-extends instead.

Datatype property

bddo:whitespaceSeparated — whitespace separated

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#whitespaceSeparated

Label
whitespace separated
Definition
If true, values are separated by a RUN of one or more whitespace bytes (space, tab, CR, LF) rather than by a fixed bddo:fieldDelimiter. A run of any length separates exactly two values, so consecutive whitespace never yields an empty value, and leading or trailing whitespace on a record is ignored. This is what column-aligned ASCII grids need -- 'ncols 100', or a row of numbers padded to line up -- which a fixed delimiter cannot express. Mutually exclusive with bddo:fieldDelimiter. When bddo:recordDelimiter is also absent the content is one flat stream of whitespace-separated values rather than rows, which is how an ASCII grid whose rows wrap across lines is read; the grid's shape then comes from the field's dlv:DataLayout or repeat count, not from line breaks.
Usage scope
Use in physical binary-format descriptions. Extents and offsets describe bytes unless a term explicitly specifies bits; HEL evaluation and failure behavior follow the processing specification. Use it as a predicate. Applicable SHACL paths and their focus-node scopes are listed below; no additional global domain is inferred from that usage.
Declared RDF type
owl:DatatypeProperty
RDFS domain
No global domain declared. SHACL usage scopes below do not create a global domain axiom.
RDFS range
xsd:boolean
Defined by
<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo>
Shape usage / validation context
bddo:DelimitedRecordsShape, bddo:DelimitedTableShape
Cardinality
Context-dependent. The linked shapes state minimum/maximum counts at their value paths; no universal cardinality is inferred.
Canonical source
bddo.ttl

  • rdf:type: owl:DatatypeProperty
  • rdfs:range: xsd:boolean
Named individual

bddo:xml — XML

IRI: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#xml

Label
XML
Definition
Paths are ABBREVIATED XPATH: child steps, attribute steps (@name), and positional predicates ([n], 1-based). A deliberately small subset -- no axes, no functions, no comparison predicates -- because a description states where a value IS, and a path that can compute is a query engine's problem rather than a description's.
Usage scope
Use in physical binary-format descriptions. Extents and offsets describe bytes unless a term explicitly specifies bits; HEL evaluation and failure behavior follow the processing specification. Use this IRI as a controlled value where the profile or a shape accepts its declared type.
Declared RDF type
owl:NamedIndividual, bddo:TreeSyntax

Canonical vocabulary and shapes

# Binary Data Description Ontology (BDDO) 1.0 — vocabulary + SHACL shapes
# Canonical machine-readable artifact. Namespace: https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#
# Modeling: properties declare rdfs:range (value type). Domain and cardinality
# are expressed in SHACL only (no rdfs:domain), to avoid over-committing subjects.
@prefix :       <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#> .
@prefix bddo:   <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#> .
@prefix owl:    <http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#> .
@prefix rdf:    <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#> .
@prefix rdfs:   <http://www.w3.org/2000/01/rdf-schema#> .
@prefix xsd:    <http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#> .
@prefix sh:     <http://www.w3.org/ns/shacl#> .
@prefix dcterms:<http://purl.org/dc/terms/> .
@prefix vann:   <http://purl.org/vocab/vann/> .

<https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> a owl:Ontology ;
    owl:versionIRI <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo/1.0> ;
    owl:versionInfo "1.0" ;
    rdfs:label "Binary Data Description Ontology (BDDO)" ;
    rdfs:comment "Vocabulary for declaratively describing the physical structure of binary data." ;
    dcterms:created "2025-08-16"^^xsd:date ;
    dcterms:creator <https://geoknoesis.com> ;
    dcterms:license <https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/> ;
    vann:preferredNamespacePrefix "bddo" ;
    vann:preferredNamespaceUri "https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo#" .

# ---------- Classes ----------
:Struct       a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Struct" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "An ordered sequence of fields; a container in a binary format." .
:Field        a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Field" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A single element within a Struct: a value, a bit slice, or an array." .
:DataType     a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Data Type" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:DataTypeRule a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Data Type Rule" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:Enumeration  a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Enumeration" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:EnumValue    a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Enumeration Value" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:Checksum     a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Checksum" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A checksum/hash over a byte range. Each END of the range is stated independently: a field reference (coversFromField/coversToField) or a HEL expression yielding a byte offset (coversFromExpression/coversToExpression). The two ends may use different mechanisms -- a checksum covering everything from a named header field to a length carried elsewhere needs exactly that. Note the boundary convention differs by mechanism, because each follows what its inputs naturally give: the field form runs from the FIRST byte of coversFromField to the LAST byte of coversToField (inclusive), while the expression form is the half-open [start,end) that the two offsets delimit." .
:EndiannessRule a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Endianness Rule" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "One arm of a hasConditionalEndianness list: a HEL condition and the byte order it selects." .
:DispatchTable a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Dispatch Table" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A keyed, open selection of a Field's effective type. The value of the key (dispatchOnField or dispatchOnExpression) is matched by equality against the armKey of the table's DispatchArms; the matching arm's armDataType is the effective type, else dispatchDefault, else the Field's dataType. Arms are independent resources rather than list members, so a description that imports this one can contribute an arm without rewriting anything. Contrast hasConditionalDataType, whose ordered predicate rules are closed and may overlap." .
:DispatchArm a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Dispatch Arm" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "One key-to-type association of a DispatchTable: the type a field takes when the table's key equals armKey. Keys are unique within a table across every graph that contributes to it." .
:SeekScope a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Seek Scope" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "How far an offset-addressed read may reach: the innermost bounded region (the default, regionScope) or the whole stream (streamScope)." .
# ---------- Delimited text records ----------
# Line- and character-delimited text: key = value headers, CSV, whitespace-separated grids.
# Records may nest, via bddo:hasGrouping, but only in the three token-delimited styles that
# vocabulary describes -- this is not a general grammar. A document with real recursive
# structure is a bddo:TreeDocument instead.
:DelimitedRecords a owl:Class ; rdfs:subClassOf :Struct ; rdfs:label "Delimited Records" ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A Struct whose children are located by delimiter position rather than byte offset: a byte run split into records by recordDelimiter, each record optionally split into values by fieldDelimiter." .
:KeyValueHeader a owl:Class ; rdfs:subClassOf :DelimitedRecords ; rdfs:label "Key/Value Header" ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "DelimitedRecords whose records are key/value pairs split by keyValueSeparator. Each expected key is a Field carrying bddo:key; record order is not significant." .
:DelimitedTable a owl:Class ; rdfs:subClassOf :DelimitedRecords ; rdfs:label "Delimited Table" ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "DelimitedRecords whose records are rows with a fixed column sequence. The hasField list gives the columns in order; the row structure repeats until the byte run is exhausted." .
# ---------- Tree documents ----------
# XML and JSON labels wrap a payload that is otherwise already describable: PDS4, DIMAP, SAFE,
# SENTINEL-2, RadarSat-2, TerraSAR-X, CPHD and Zarr all pair a clean binary array with a
# manifest BDDO could not read, and the whole GML/KML/GeoJSON family is markup end to end.
#
# BDDO does not describe the SYNTAX of these documents -- it addresses nodes in the tree a
# conforming parser produces. That distinction is what keeps this from becoming an XML
# specification: the tree is someone else's output, and BDDO's job is only to say which node
# supplies which field.
:TreeDocument a owl:Class ; rdfs:subClassOf :Struct ; rdfs:label "Tree Document" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A Struct whose children are located by a path into a parsed node tree rather than by byte offset or delimiter. The byte run is handed to a parser for the declared syntax; each Field names the node supplying its value." .
:TreeSyntax a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Tree Syntax" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "Which tree grammar a TreeDocument is written in, and therefore how its nodePath values are read." .
:NamespaceBinding a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Namespace Binding" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "A prefix-to-IRI binding for the paths of an XML TreeDocument." .

:RecordGrouping a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Record Grouping" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "How a DelimitedRecords container nests: which records open a group, which close it, and where the group's name comes from. A container without one is flat, which is what every DelimitedRecords was before nesting existed." .
:GroupingStyle a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Grouping Style" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "Which of the three ways a text format marks a nested group." .

# Controlled-vocabulary classes (typed enumerated individuals)
:Endianness       a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Endianness" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:BitOrder         a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Bit Order" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:BaseType         a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Base Type" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:OffsetBase       a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Offset Base" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:Encoding         a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Encoding" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:ChecksumAlgorithm a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Checksum Algorithm" ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
# Union of DataType and Struct, used as the range of dataType / ruleDataType
:DataTypeOrStruct a owl:Class ; rdfs:label "Data Type or Struct" ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ; owl:unionOf ( :DataType :Struct ) .

# ---------- Structural properties ----------
:hasField     a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "has field" ; rdfs:range rdf:List ;
    rdfs:comment "Ordered rdf:List of the Fields that make up a Struct." .
:endianness   a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "endianness" ; rdfs:range :Endianness ;
    rdfs:comment "Byte order for a Struct, Field, or DataType." .
:hasConditionalEndianness a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "has conditional endianness" ; rdfs:range rdf:List ;
    rdfs:comment "Ordered rdf:List of EndiannessRule. The first rule whose condition holds selects the byte order for this Struct or Field and, as with bddo:endianness, for its descendants unless they override it. Use for formats that declare their own byte order, such as TIFF's 4949h/4D4Dh marker. Mutually exclusive with bddo:endianness." .
:bitOrder     a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "bit order" ; rdfs:range :BitOrder .
:syncOnMarker a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "sync on marker" ; rdfs:range xsd:hexBinary .

# ---------- Field properties ----------
:dataType               a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "data type" ; rdfs:range :DataTypeOrStruct .
:hasConditionalDataType a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "has conditional data type" ; rdfs:range rdf:List .
:hasDispatchTable       a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "has dispatch table" ; rdfs:range :DispatchTable ;
    rdfs:comment "The keyed DispatchTable that selects this Field's effective type. Mutually exclusive with hasConditionalDataType." .
:dispatchOnField        a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "dispatch on field" ; rdfs:range :Field ;
    rdfs:comment "The sibling Field whose parsed value is the dispatch key." .
:dispatchOnExpression   a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "dispatch on expression" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "A HEL expression whose value is the dispatch key, evaluated as a hasConditionalDataType condition is." .
:dispatchDefault        a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "dispatch default" ; rdfs:range :DataTypeOrStruct ;
    rdfs:comment "The type selected when no arm's key equals the dispatch key. Absent means the Field's own dataType applies." .
:armTable               a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "arm table" ; rdfs:range :DispatchTable ;
    rdfs:comment "The DispatchTable this DispatchArm contributes to. Declared on the arm so that any graph may add arms to a table defined elsewhere." .
:armKey                 a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "arm key" ;
    rdfs:comment "The key value this arm matches: an xsd:string compared by code points, or an xsd:integer compared numerically." .
:armDataType            a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "arm data type" ; rdfs:range :DataTypeOrStruct ;
    rdfs:comment "The datatype or structure selected when this arm's key matches." .
:seekScope              a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "seek scope" ; rdfs:range :SeekScope ;
    rdfs:comment "For an offset-addressed Field, whether the read may leave the innermost bounded region (streamScope) or is confined to it (regionScope, the default). The region bound is suspended only for that one read. Used where a table inside one length-delimited container locates payloads stored in another." .
:size                   a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "size (bytes)" ; rdfs:range xsd:positiveInteger .
:sizeFromField          a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "size from field" ; rdfs:range :Field .
:sizeFromExpression     a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "size from expression" ; rdfs:range xsd:string .
:sizeToEndOfStream      a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "size to end of stream" ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean .
:terminator             a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "terminator" ; rdfs:range xsd:hexBinary .
:trimNull               a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "trim null" ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean ;
    rdfs:comment "For a fixed-size (padded) string field, the emitted value ends at the first 0x00 byte within the field's width." .
:encoding               a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "encoding" ; rdfs:range :Encoding .
:recordDelimiter        a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "record delimiter" ; rdfs:range xsd:hexBinary ;
    rdfs:comment "Byte sequence separating records. Default 0A (LF); a CR LF pair is also accepted when this is 0A." .
:fieldDelimiter         a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "field delimiter" ; rdfs:range xsd:hexBinary ;
    rdfs:comment "Byte sequence separating values within one record. When absent, a record is a single value." .
:keyValueSeparator      a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "key/value separator" ; rdfs:range xsd:hexBinary ;
    rdfs:comment "Byte sequence separating a key from its value within a record of a KeyValueHeader." .
:quoteChar              a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "quote character" ; rdfs:range xsd:hexBinary ;
    rdfs:comment "Byte that quotes a value, allowing it to contain the field or record delimiter. A doubled quote inside a quoted value denotes a literal quote." .
:escapeChar             a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "escape character" ; rdfs:range xsd:hexBinary ;
    rdfs:comment "Byte that escapes the following byte, suppressing its delimiter or quote meaning." .
:commentPrefix          a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "comment prefix" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "A record beginning with this string (after optional whitespace) is ignored." .
:skipRecords            a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "skip records" ; rdfs:range xsd:nonNegativeInteger ;
    rdfs:comment "Number of leading records to discard before parsing, e.g. a CSV column-name row. Default 0." .
:whitespaceSeparated    a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "whitespace separated" ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean ;
    rdfs:comment "If true, values are separated by a RUN of one or more whitespace bytes (space, tab, CR, LF) rather than by a fixed bddo:fieldDelimiter. A run of any length separates exactly two values, so consecutive whitespace never yields an empty value, and leading or trailing whitespace on a record is ignored. This is what column-aligned ASCII grids need -- 'ncols        100', or a row of numbers padded to line up -- which a fixed delimiter cannot express. Mutually exclusive with bddo:fieldDelimiter. When bddo:recordDelimiter is also absent the content is one flat stream of whitespace-separated values rather than rows, which is how an ASCII grid whose rows wrap across lines is read; the grid's shape then comes from the field's dlv:DataLayout or repeat count, not from line breaks." .
:trimWhitespace         a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "trim whitespace" ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean ;
    rdfs:comment "If true, leading and trailing whitespace is removed from every key and value. Default false." .
# ---------- Tree document properties ----------
:treeSyntax a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "tree syntax" ; rdfs:range :TreeSyntax ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The grammar of a TreeDocument. Required, because it decides how every nodePath in the container is read." .
:xml a owl:NamedIndividual, :TreeSyntax ; rdfs:label "XML" ;
    rdfs:comment "Paths are ABBREVIATED XPATH: child steps, attribute steps (@name), and positional predicates ([n], 1-based). A deliberately small subset -- no axes, no functions, no comparison predicates -- because a description states where a value IS, and a path that can compute is a query engine's problem rather than a description's." .
:json a owl:NamedIndividual, :TreeSyntax ; rdfs:label "JSON" ;
    rdfs:comment "Paths are JSON POINTERS as defined by RFC 6901: /object/member, array indices as /list/0 (0-based). Adopted rather than invented, so its escaping rules for '/' and '~' inside a member name are already specified and already implemented." .

:nodePath a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "node path" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "Locates the node supplying this field's value, read according to the container's treeSyntax. A path selecting no node leaves the field absent; a path selecting several is an error unless the field repeats, in which case the field's value is the selected nodes in document order." .
:hasNamespaceBinding a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "has namespace binding" ; rdfs:range :NamespaceBinding ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "Binds the prefixes used in this document's paths. XML only, and not optional in practice: PDS4, DIMAP and SAFE are all namespaced, and an unprefixed path against a namespaced document silently matches nothing rather than failing." .
:namespacePrefix a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "namespace prefix" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The prefix as used in this container's nodePath values. The empty string binds the DEFAULT namespace, which an XPath subset cannot otherwise reach." .
:namespaceIRI a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "namespace IRI" ; rdfs:range xsd:anyURI ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The namespace the prefix stands for, matched against the document's own declarations rather than against the prefix it happens to use -- a document may spell the same namespace with any prefix." .

# ---------- Nested records ----------
# The flat key/value model reaches ESRI .hdr, ENVI and their kin, but stops at the label formats
# that are otherwise the same shape: PVL/ODL (PDS, ISIS2, ISIS3, VICAR) nests with
# OBJECT = ... END_OBJECT, ERMapper with <Name> Begin ... <Name> End, and INI-style labels with
# bracketed section headers. Refusing to nest kept nine drivers at payload-only for want of a
# closing token.
#
# Three styles rather than one general grammar. Each is fully described by two tokens plus where
# the group's NAME comes from, and that is the whole of it -- a general nested-grammar surface
# would be a parser generator, and this is not one.
:hasGrouping a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "has grouping" ; rdfs:range :RecordGrouping ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The nesting rule of a DelimitedRecords container. Absent means flat." .
:groupingStyle a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "grouping style" ; rdfs:range :GroupingStyle ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> .
:groupOpenToken a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "group open token" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The literal that marks a record as opening a group: the key OBJECT, the value Begin, or the bracket [ -- read according to groupingStyle." .
:groupCloseToken a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "group close token" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The literal that closes a group: END_OBJECT, End, or the bracket ]. For sectionHeaded it is the closing bracket of the header rather than a separate record, since a section is ended by the next one." .

:keyedGroup a owl:NamedIndividual, :GroupingStyle ; rdfs:label "keyed group" ;
    rdfs:comment "A record whose KEY equals groupOpenToken opens a group, and its VALUE names it; a record whose key equals groupCloseToken closes the innermost open group. PVL/ODL: OBJECT = IMAGE ... END_OBJECT = IMAGE. The closing record MAY repeat the name; a description need not say so, because the name is already known from the opening record." .
:valuedGroup a owl:NamedIndividual, :GroupingStyle ; rdfs:label "valued group" ;
    rdfs:comment "The mirror image: a record whose VALUE equals groupOpenToken opens a group, and its KEY names it. ERMapper: CoordinateSpace Begin ... CoordinateSpace End." .
:sectionHeaded a owl:NamedIndividual, :GroupingStyle ; rdfs:label "section headed" ;
    rdfs:comment "A record consisting of groupOpenToken, a name, and groupCloseToken opens a group and implicitly closes the previous one; the last runs to the end of the container. INI-style [Section] headers. Nesting is therefore FLAT ONE DEEP -- sections do not contain sections -- which is what the style actually permits, not a limitation of this model." .

:keyPath a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "key path" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "Locates a field inside nested groups, as group names and finally the key, joined by pathSeparator: 'IMAGE/BAND/NAME'. Mutually exclusive with bddo:key, which locates a field in a flat container -- a field cannot be found two ways, and a description that said both would leave a reader to guess which wins." .
:pathSeparator a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "path separator" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ; rdfs:isDefinedBy <https://hexplain.io/ns/bddo> ;
    rdfs:comment "The separator used in this container's keyPath values. Default '/'. Declared on the container rather than repeated per field, and needed because a format whose group names may contain '/' has to choose something else." .

:key                    a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "key" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "The key naming this Field within a KeyValueHeader. Replaces positional location: the Field takes the value of whichever record carries this key." .
:keyIsCaseInsensitive   a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "key is case insensitive" ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean ;
    rdfs:comment "If true, bddo:key matching ignores ASCII case. Default false." .
:numericBase            a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "numeric base" ; rdfs:range xsd:positiveInteger ;
    rdfs:comment "Radix used to read an :asciiInteger field: 8, 10 or 16. Default 10." .
:bitLength              a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "bit length" ; rdfs:range xsd:positiveInteger .
:repeatCount            a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "repeat count" ; rdfs:range xsd:nonNegativeInteger .
:repeatCountFromField   a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "repeat count from field" ; rdfs:range :Field .
:repeatCountFromExpression a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "repeat count from expression" ; rdfs:range xsd:string .
:repeatUntil            a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "repeat until" ; rdfs:range xsd:string .
:atOffset               a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "at offset" ; rdfs:range xsd:nonNegativeInteger .
:atOffsetFromField      a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "at offset from field" ; rdfs:range :Field .
:atOffsetFromExpression a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "at offset from expression" ; rdfs:range xsd:string .
:offsetBase             a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "offset base" ; rdfs:range :OffsetBase .
:alignment              a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "alignment" ; rdfs:range xsd:positiveInteger .
:hasFixedValue          a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "has fixed value" .
:isPresentIf            a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "is present if" ; rdfs:range xsd:string .
:valueFromExpression    a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "value from expression" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "A HEL expression giving the value of a derived, zero-byte field. The field consumes no bytes; its computed value is placed in the parse context under the field's name so later sizing, offset, presence, mapping and validation expressions may reference it. A field with bddo:valueFromExpression declares no bddo:dataType and no sizing, offset or bit-length; the result's type is that of the HEL expression." .
:validIf                a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "valid if" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "A HEL boolean expression that MUST hold once this field is parsed; if it evaluates false the parse fails with a validation error. Use for post-parse constraints such as a magic number or a bound on a count. Richer, graph-level constraints belong in SHACL at the semantic layer." .
:enumeration            a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "enumeration" ; rdfs:range :Enumeration .
:checksum               a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "checksum" ; rdfs:range :Checksum .

# ---------- DataType / Rule / Enum / Checksum properties ----------
:baseType      a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "base type" ; rdfs:range :BaseType .
:bitWidth      a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "bit width" ; rdfs:range xsd:positiveInteger .
:isSigned      a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "is signed" ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean .
:xsdType       a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "xsd type" ; rdfs:range rdfs:Datatype ;
    rdfs:comment "The RDF literal datatype produced when a value of this DataType is emitted." .
:condition     a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "condition" ; rdfs:range xsd:string .
:ruleDataType  a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "rule data type" ; rdfs:range :DataTypeOrStruct .
:ruleEndianness a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "rule endianness" ; rdfs:range :Endianness ;
    rdfs:comment "The byte order selected by an EndiannessRule." .
:hasEnumValue  a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "has enum value" ; rdfs:range :EnumValue .
:enumRawValue  a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "enum raw value" .
:enumSymbol    a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "enum symbol" .
:enumIsFlags   a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "enum is flags" ; rdfs:range xsd:boolean ;
    rdfs:comment "If true, the Enumeration is a bit-flag set: each EnumValue's raw value is a bitmask, and every symbol whose bit(s) are set in the parsed value is emitted." .
:checksumAlgorithm a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "checksum algorithm" ; rdfs:range :ChecksumAlgorithm .
:coversFromField   a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "covers from field" ; rdfs:range :Field .
:coversToField     a owl:ObjectProperty ; rdfs:label "covers to field" ; rdfs:range :Field .
# A single expression can only describe a range it computes whole, so a checksum whose start
# is a named field and whose end is computed had no way to say so -- it had to abandon the
# field reference and recompute that offset arithmetically too. Splitting coverage into two
# independent bounds removes that all-or-nothing choice; either end may be a field or an
# expression. Offsets are absolute byte positions in the same frame of reference a size or
# offset expression on this struct would see.
:coversFromExpression a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "covers from expression" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "HEL expression evaluating to the covered range's START byte offset, inclusive. Alternative to coversFromField for the same bound." .
:coversToExpression a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "covers to expression" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    rdfs:comment "HEL expression evaluating to the covered range's END byte offset, EXCLUSIVE (one past the last covered byte). Alternative to coversToField for the same bound -- note coversToField names a field whose last byte is included, so the two forms do not take the same number." .
:coversExpression  a owl:DatatypeProperty ; rdfs:label "covers expression" ; rdfs:range xsd:string ;
    owl:deprecated true ;
    rdfs:comment "Deprecated: a single expression yielding the whole [start,end) range. Superseded by the coversFromExpression/coversToExpression pair, which states each bound separately and so can mix field and expression ends. Retained for descriptions written against earlier BDDO." .
